## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route: nine numbered sections with colour-coded dividers, a contents page, and a closing forecasts slide and call to action. Most content slides pair a claim headline with one chart, and highlighted key phrases make each takeaway easy to find, as on slides 5, 8 and 19. The system is applied consistently across 54 pages, with annotated charts, a highlighted row on the table in slide 19, and direct labels. Weaknesses are a title slide where a purple and blue block overlaps the word STATE, a disclosures page of tiny text, and several dense or tiny-text slides (9, 38, 39, 40, 48) that need a reading setting rather than a live room.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the claim-headline plus single-chart pattern, the highlighter emphasis on key phrases, and the annotated charts on slides 5, 8 and 19, which carry the takeaway visually.

**Most useful repair:** Fix the overlapping block that obscures the word STATE on slide 1, and enlarge or split the tiny-text slides, especially the ZK timeline on slide 39, the privacy slide 38 and the legal slide 48.

**What would change the score:** I would cut the long disclosures text on slide 2 to a short notice with a link, and simplify the multi-panel slides (9, 21, 45) so each carries one chart.

_Confidence high. 54 of 54 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide, a full-page disclosures slide, and a contents page listing nine sections. It moves through the crypto market's size and cycles, institutional adoption by banks, asset managers and payment firms, the growth of stablecoins, applications such as decentralized exchanges, real-world assets and prediction markets, blockchain infrastructure and throughput, the convergence of crypto with AI, the US regulatory environment, and a preview of a live dashboard. Each section opens with a numbered divider slide, and most content slides pair a headline-style claim with a single chart or table. The closing sections give a list of nine forecasts for 2025 and beyond, followed by a call-to-action slide linking to the podcast, newsletter and social channels. Slides worth studying include the market cycle chart on slide 5, which annotates events directly on the data, the stablecoin payment comparison on slide 19, and the Bitcoin versus macro benchmarks chart on slide 8, where a highlighted correlation box carries the takeaway.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the a16z crypto State of Crypto 2025 deck say about the cost and speed of stablecoin payments compared with other U.S. payment products?**

Slide 19 says stablecoins are one of the cheapest ways to send a dollar, with a stablecoin transfer on Solana or Ethereum L2s costing less than $0.01 and settling in less than 1 second. By comparison, the slide lists an ACH transfer at $0.20 to $1.50 settling in 3 to 5 business days.

**How many slides does the a16z crypto State of Crypto 2025 deck have, and how is it structured?**

The deck has 54 pages. Slide 3 is the contents page, listing nine sections from the crypto market's size through to what is next for crypto.

**What does the a16z crypto State of Crypto 2025 deck say about stablecoin supply and its projected growth?**

Slide 23 gives stablecoin supply today as $326 billion, citing Allium as of 9/30/2025. It also shows a projected stablecoin supply by 2030 of $1.6 to $3.7 trillion, attributed to Citi.

**What does the a16z crypto State of Crypto 2025 deck say about institutional capital in Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs?**

Slide 14 states that Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs hold over $175 billion in onchain assets. The slide breaks this down as $150 billion in Bitcoin ETF holdings and $28 billion in Ethereum ETF holdings.

**Layout**

Two-column split: a left text column (about 2% to 24% of width) holds the kicker and a large headline set flush left with the top; the right column (about 30% to 98%) stacks three time-aligned charts (market cap, developers, mobile wallet users) sharing one x-axis of years 2016 to 2025, with the sources line and copyright line along the bottom footer and the a16zcrypto logo bottom-left; the kicker sits top-left at about 3.6% from top and the left margin is about 2.3%.

**Visuals**

Three stacked charts sharing an x-axis from 2016 to 2025. (1) Line chart titled Crypto market cap¹, y-axis $0 to $5T with gridlines at $1T steps, dark navy #0B1F3A line that rises from near $0 in 2016 to a peak of about $4.2T at the right edge, with local peaks about $0.9T in early 2018, about $2.5T in late 2021, about $3.1T in early 2022, and about $3.9T in 2025; no legend. Highlighted periods are beige-grey bands (#E4E1CC) with dotted black borders and orange tops labelled ICO boom (2017 to 2018), DeFi summer (2021 to 2021.5), NFT surge (2021.5 to 2022.4), Bitcoin ETF (2024 to 2024.4), Memecoin mania (2025 to 2025.2); a black arrow at the right end points right beyond the 2025 mark. (2) Bar chart titled Crypto developers², y-axis 0 to 40K with gridlines at 10K steps, royal blue #1A5BE0 bars, monthly from 2016 to 2025; values about 3K in 2016 rising to about 8K in 2018, about 14K in 2019, about 14K in 2020, about 20K in 2021, about 33K in 2022 peak, about 28K to 30K in 2023 to 2024, about 25K to 33K in 2025. Highlighted bands repeat from the top chart. (3) Bar chart titled Crypto mobile wallet users³, y-axis 0 to 60M with gridlines at 20M steps, violet #7B2FF7 bars; near 0 until 2020, about 2M in early 2021, about 13M in mid 2021, about 22M in 2022 to 2023, about 25M in 2024, rising to about 45M to 50M in 2025 with a peak near 50M. Two orange-red VIEW LIVE buttons (#F05A28) sit beside the developer and wallet chart titles. Three rounded callout boxes overlay the charts: blue DeFi and NFTs callout, blue Regulation and stablecoins callout, purple ETFs and memecoins callout.

Background: Warm off-white #F2F0E8 solid fill. ©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

**Layout**

Two-column composition on a warm off-white field: a wide chart occupies the left roughly two-thirds with its title top-left under a small orange section label, and a large headline block sits in the right third, left-aligned, top-aligned with the chart title. A footer row runs along the bottom with the logo at left, sources line beside it, and copyright at right; there is no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Multi-series line chart titled 'Bitcoin price vs. macro benchmarks'. Four series over time from about 2019 to late 2025: Bitcoin (orange, jagged, rising from about $5K-$10K in 2019 to peaks near $70K in 2021, a dip to about $20K in 2022, and a rise to about $110K-$120K in 2025), Nasdaq (purple, rising from about 2019 index-equivalent of $5K-$10K scale to about $20K-$25K scale by 2025, with a peak near 2021 and a dip in 2022), Gold (blue, relatively flat in 2019-2024 then rising to about $4K-$5K scale by 2025), and Fed Funds Target Rate (dark navy stepped line, from about 0% in 2020-2022 to about 5% peak in 2023-2024 and stepping down to about 4.5% in 2025). Left axes are unlabelled in-chart; right-hand value scales read: Bitcoin $0 to $125K in steps of $25K, Nasdaq $0 to $25K, Gold $0 to $5K, Fed rate 0% to 5% in 1% steps. Horizontal light grey gridlines at each step. A shaded olive-beige rectangle with dotted border highlights the 2025 region, containing the Pearson correlation box: 'Pearson Correlation (2025)5', 'BTC/Gold ~0.06', 'BTC/Nasdaq ~0.47'. Legend beneath chart with four entries: orange Bitcoin1, purple Nasdaq2, blue Gold3, navy Fed Funds Target Rate4. No photographs or icons apart from the small logo.

**Design**

Background: Warm off-white #F2F0E8 solid colour. Warm off-white background with near-black text. Orange #F7941D accent for section label, title highlight and Bitcoin series. Headline in heavy geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style), roughly 4.5% of slide height per line with tight leading; chart title bold sans about 2.6% of height; body and labels regular sans about 1.6% of height. Headline 'Macro forces matter' has an orange #F7941D highlight block behind the words, with black text. Thin light grey gridlines #D9D6CC. Shaded highlight rectangle fill olive-beige #E4E1CC with dotted black border. Series colours: Bitcoin orange #F7941D, Nasdaq violet #8A2BE2, Gold blue #1F5FD6, Fed rate navy #14213D. No shadows or gradients; no rounded corners except the small orange square bullet before the section label.

**Layout**

Two equal columns below a full-width two-line title, with the kicker above the title, all left-aligned to a margin of about 2.3% of width. The left column holds a stacked area chart with its subtitle and footnote 1; the right column holds a table with its subtitle and footnote 2, aligned to the same top edge. Footnote 2 sits under the table, the a16zcrypto logo sits bottom-left, and the copyright line sits bottom-right; there is no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Left: a 100% stacked area chart titled by its subtitle 'Mobile wallet usage, an indicator of onchain activity, comes largely from developing countries'. Y axis labelled 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100% (share of monthly active mobile crypto wallet users); X axis labelled 2018, 2020, 2022, 2024, running to about 2025 with no unit label. Series from top to bottom of the stack: Other (grey-blue, about 35% to 40% at 2018 rising to about 33% to 40% at the end, the largest band throughout), Peru, Indonesia, Pakistan, Philippines, Thailand, Argentina, Colombia, India, Nigeria, United States (navy, bottom band, about 40% at 2018, dropping to about 12% to 18% in 2020 to 2025, peaking near 30% in 2021). Nigeria (bright blue) is the second band from the bottom, about 10 to 20 points thick in 2020 to 2024. India (orange) is about 5 to 10 points thick in 2021 to 2024. Colombia (yellow) appears from about 2023, about 5 to 8 points. Philippines (magenta) peaks near 10 points around 2021 to 2022. Legend sits to the right of the plot as a vertical list with coloured squares, in the order listed. No highlighted points. Right: a table with four columns and sixteen rows including a header, described in the text field. The last column is shaded on a red-to-green scale, green for the highest values (Australia, South Korea), yellow in the middle (United States, Spain, Germany, France), orange for Vietnam, Russia, Indonesia, Turkey, and red for Nigeria, Brazil, India.

Background: Warm off-white #F2EFE6 solid colour. **Layout**

The a16zcrypto logo sits bottom left and the copyright line sits bottom right on the same baseline, with no slide number.

**Visuals**

Two horizontal bar charts with logo badges. Left chart: title 'Top blockchains by builder interest' with superscript 1 and a 'VIEW LIVE' button; value axis 0% to 20% with gridlines at 5% intervals (0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%); single series in blue bars, sorted descending. About values: Ethereum about 23.5%, Base about 17.3%, Solana about 16.9%, Bitcoin about 4.9%, Polygon about 4.9%, Arbitrum about 4.4%, Optimism about 2.6%, Hyperliquid about 2.6%, Avalanche about 2.1%, TON about 2.1%. Right chart: title 'Top blockchains by monthly open source developers' with superscript 2; value axis 0 to 7.5K with ticks at 0, 2.5K, 5K, 7.5K; single series in purple bars, sorted descending. About values: Ethereum about 9,100, Solana about 3,850, Bitcoin about 2,500, Base about 1,550, Polygon about 1,480, Cosmos about 1,300, Polkadot about 1,000, Arbitrum about 980, Sui about 750, BNB Chain about 650. No values are printed; all are estimates. Logos: Ethereum diamond, Solana three bars, Bitcoin B, Base square, Polygon links, Cosmos atom-like symbol, Polkadot ring, Arbitrum A, Sui droplet, BNB Chain cube. Light vertical gridlines are drawn behind both charts; the zero axis is a dark vertical line where the logos sit.

Background: Solid warm off-white / cream #F1EFE8. Warm off-white background with orange kicker and orange highlight band behind the second headline line. The title sits top-left under the kicker, all text is left-aligned, margins are about 2 percent, and no slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical stacked bar chart of weekly net flows in USD billions, titled 'Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs: Weekly net flows (USD)' with footnote marker 1. Y-axis gridlines labelled $4B, $2B, $0, -$2B and -$4B; the $0 line is a darker black baseline and the other gridlines are light grey. X-axis labels are Mar 2024, Jun 2024, Sep 2024, Dec 2024, Mar 2025, Jun 2025 and Sep 2025, spaced about every three months. Series: Bitcoin ETFs in orange #F7941D as the lower stacked segment and Ethereum ETFs in purple #D16BFF as the upper segment; bars are about 1 percent of width with rounded tops, and values are about read from the bars. Approximate notable values: a tall positive week in about Dec 2024 reaching about +3.6B total, a peak in about Sep 2025 reaching about +3.9B with about +2.9B purple, and a deep negative week in about Mar 2025 reaching about -2.9B with about -0.3B purple. No values are printed on the bars and no highlighted points are marked. Legend below the chart: orange square Bitcoin ETFs, purple square Ethereum ETFs. Two overlaid rounded callout boxes sit over the lower-left chart area: an orange box reading Bitcoin ETF Holdings / $150 billion and a purple box reading Ethereum ETF Holdings / $28 billion, both white bold text. A 'VIEW LIVE' orange-red pill button with a white arrow icon and underlined white bold text sits next to the chart title. The a16z crypto logo is a black wordmark at bottom left.

**Layout**

Full-bleed flat slide with no grid. A maroon tab is pinned to the top-left corner, with the section label inside it. A large overlapping '03' graphic fills the upper-right quadrant and bleeds toward the right margin. The footer has the logo at bottom left and the copyright line right-aligned at bottom right, both about 4.5% from the bottom edge. There is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Decorative graphic at upper right: the numerals '03' in a heavy rounded sans, set large and overlapping. The '0' and '3' are drawn in two solid tones: a bright blue (#4B3BFF) fill and a dark navy (#0B1A3A) fill. Where the two overlap they form a deeper translucent navy (#152A66). The blue and navy shapes are interlocked with stepped, notched rectangular blocks behind the digits, so the mass reads as a stepped blue plinth. No photograph, chart or icon. The only logo is the a16zcrypto wordmark at bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid light blue #A3C8FF. Flat light-blue background with no gradient or texture. Headline and footer text are dark navy #0B1A3A. The section label is cream #F5EFE6 on a deep maroon #4A1530 tab. The numeral graphic uses bright blue #4B3BFF and navy #0B1A3A. The section label is about 4.6% of slide height. The copyright and logo are small, at about 2% of slide height. Shapes have softly rounded corners of about 6px at the scale of the slide and no shadows. The maroon tab is an irregular notched shape: a wide top bar with a narrower step cut out below it at the left.

### Slide 19

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A clean comparison table with the fastest and cheapest option quietly emphasised in the table's own styling makes the argument without extra text.
- Review: Most content slides pair a claim headline with one chart, and highlighted key phrases make each takeaway easy to find, as on slides 5, 8 and 19.
- Review: The system is applied consistently across 54 pages, with annotated charts, a highlighted row on the table in slide 19, and direct labels.
- Worth keeping: Keep the claim-headline plus single-chart pattern, the highlighter emphasis on key phrases, and the annotated charts on slides 5, 8 and 19, which carry the takeaway visually.

**Text**

STABLECOINS / Stablecoins are / one of the / cheapest ways to / send a dollar / — in less than 1 / second for less / than 1 cent / Payment products in the U.S. / Payment type / Transaction fee / Time to settle / Credit card payment / 2 – 3% + $0.30 / Instant to merchant / Debit card payment / Durbin: 0.05% + $0.21 / Non-Durbin: 0.9% + $0.15 / Instant to merchant / ACH / $0.20 – $1.50 / 3 – 5 business days / International wire transfer / $30 – $50 / 1 – 5 business days / Remittance service / 6.65% (for $200) / Minutes to days / Peer-to-peer payment app / Free (p2p) / 1 – 3% (business) / Instant to 1 day / Stablecoin transfer / (Solana, Ethereum L2s) / < $0.01 / < 1 second / Source: a16z crypto analysis / a16zcrypto / ©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a cream field: a narrow left column holds the kicker and a large bold headline set flush left at the top margin, and a wide right column holds a single full-height data table starting at the same top line as the headline. The footer runs along the bottom margin with the a16zcrypto logo at left, the source line aligned to the table's left edge, and the copyright line right-aligned to the table's right edge; no slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

One table, titled 'Payment products in the U.S.', with 3 columns (Payment type, Transaction fee, Time to settle) and 8 body rows after a navy merged title row. Rows in order: Credit card payment | 2 – 3% + $0.30 | Instant to merchant. Debit card payment | Durbin: 0.05% + $0.21 / Non-Durbin: 0.9% + $0.15 | Instant to merchant. ACH | $0.20 – $1.50 | 3 – 5 business days. International wire transfer | $30 – $50 | 1 – 5 business days. Remittance service | 6.65% (for $200) | Minutes to days. Peer-to-peer payment app | Free (p2p) / 1 – 3% (business) | Instant to 1 day. Highlighted final row, Stablecoin transfer (Solana, Ethereum L2s) | < $0.01 | < 1 second, in white bold text on bright blue. No chart, photo, or logo other than the a16zcrypto wordmark. The pink highlight behind part of the headline is a text highlight, not a visual element. Table cell borders are thin dark navy lines.

**Design**

Background: Solid cream #F1EFE8 across the whole slide. Warm off-white cream background. Kicker and headline in near-black #111111 (kicker in deep maroon #4A0E2E with a small maroon square marker). Headline is heavy geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style) with tight leading, about 5% of slide height per line. Table header title row is navy #0A2157 with white bold sans text; column header row is light periwinkle blue #A8CBFA with bold near-black text; body rows are off-white #FDFDF8 with near-black regular sans text about 2.3% of slide height; the final row is electric blue #4B3BFF with white bold text. Headline highlight is dusty pink #E0A0AA behind 'less than 1', 'second for less' and 'than 1 cent'. Footer text is small black sans about 1.5% of slide height. Table borders are 1px dark navy #0A1F4A lines; no rounded corners, shadows or gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 2.3, y 3.7, w 1, h 1.4 [#4A0E2E]: Kicker marker: small rounded square in deep maroon (Corner radius about 15% of its width; sits left of the kicker text)

**Layout**

Single slide with a large left-aligned headline across the top spanning nearly the full width, a line chart with bar overlay occupying the left ~50% below the headline, and a rounded beige panel on the right ~40% containing a pie-style stat block and two callout boxes. **Visuals**

Combined chart: line and bar chart. Title: Foreign central bank reserves and U.S. national debt. Left y-axis 0% to 80% in 20% steps (percent of foreign reserves). Right y-axis $0 to $40T in $10T steps (U.S. national debt in trillions of dollars). X-axis years 1970 to about 2025 with labels every 10 years. Series: Gold holdings as a % of foreign reserves (light purple/orchid line) peaks about 74% near 1980, falls to about 5% near 2015 and rises to about 21% by 2025. U.S. Treasuries holdings as a % of foreign reserves (blue line) starts about 13% in 1970, peaks about 29% in 1972, dips to about 2% near 1980, rises to about 28% near 2015 and ends about 18% in 2025. U.S. national debt (pale blue bars) rises from near $0 in 1970 to about $37T in 2025. A dotted black rectangle highlights the 2023-2025 bars at the right edge of the chart. **Visuals**

Stacked bar chart titled 'Prediction market trading volume', with monthly bars from Jan 2024 to about Sep 2025 on the horizontal axis (labelled Jan 2024, Jul 2024, Jan 2025, Jul 2025) and trading volume in US dollars on the vertical axis ranging from $0 to about $1.8B (gridlines labelled $0, $500M, $1B, $1.5B). Each bar is stacked into four series: Polymarket in green (bottom), Kalshi in dark slate, Limitless in orange (thin top segment), and Myriad in purple (not visibly reaching bars). Values are not printed; estimates: the bars rise from near zero in early 2024, reach about $440M in Oct 2024, peak at about $1.8B around the US presidential election in Nov 2024 (Polymarket about $1.02B, Kalshi about $0.75B), fall to about $350M to $500M through early 2025, and climb to about $1.25B to $1.3B at the latest bars near Sep 2025 (Polymarket about $410M, Kalshi about $0.85B, Limitless about $0.05B). Callout box 'U.S. presidential election' with white fill points to the peak bar; callout box 'NFL season begins' with white fill sits above the final bars at right. Legend below the chart in one row: green square Polymarket, dark slate square Kalshi, orange square Limitless, purple square Myriad. Gridlines are thin light grey horizontal lines.

Callout boxes are white #FFFFFF rectangles with rounded corners and bold black text. Gridlines light grey #C8C5BC, thin. No shadows or gradients.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a cream background with a full-width headline spanning the top. A small pink section kicker sits top-left above a two-line bold title, which is left-aligned at a margin of about 2.3% of width. The left column holds a dual-axis line and area chart, the right column a stacked bar chart, both with subtitles above their plots. The bottom strip holds the a16zcrypto logo and source line at left, and the copyright notice flush right, with no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Left chart: dual-axis combination chart titled Non-vote transactions per second (TPS) and average transaction fee on Solana. Left y-axis Non-vote TPS from 0 to 1,500 transactions per second, gridlines at 500, 1,000 and 1,500. Right y-axis Average transaction fee in US dollars from $0.00 to $0.03, labelled $0.00, $0.01, $0.02, $0.03 with the latest value marked as bold $0.00433. X-axis years 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 with the series running monthly from early 2021 to late 2025. Series 1, Non-vote TPS: light lavender filled area (about #C9A8FA) with a peak near 760 in late 2021, lows near 150 in 2021 and 2023, about 880 in early 2024, a sharp rise to about 1,190 in early 2025, and about 1,340 peak in late 2025 before falling to about 930. Series 2, Average transaction fee: dark violet line (about #7B1FE0) that stays near $0.00 through 2021 to 2023, rises to about $0.011 in early 2024, falls to about $0.006, spikes to about $0.020 in late 2024 and about $0.021 in early 2025, then drops to about $0.004 and settles near $0.00433. Legend below the chart: lavender square labelled Non-vote TPS¹ and violet line labelled Average transaction fee². Right chart: stacked bar chart titled Application revenue¹ on Solana. Y-axis is revenue in US dollars millions from $0 to $800M with gridlines at $200M, $400M, $600M and $800M. X-axis years 2023, 2024, 2025, with about 40 quarterly bars from 2023 through late 2025. Values are about: bars near zero through 2023, small bars in early 2024 (under $10M), rising to about $80M in mid 2024, about $190M in late 2024 peak around $215M, about $620M in early 2025 peak (tallest bar), about $240M, then between about $100M and $200M through late 2025. Stack segments from bottom to top: Memecoins (near-black navy), Trading tools (blue), DEX (salmon pink), Wallet (red-orange), MEV (lime green), Lending (dark green), Launchpad (violet), Other (grey-blue). Largest segment in the peak bar is Trading tools (about $250M) and Memecoins (about $145M). No highlighted points. Legend box at top right of chart, listing the eight segments top to bottom as Other, Launchpad, Lending, MEV, Wallet, DEX, Trading tools, Memecoins with coloured squares. Footer logo a16zcrypto wordmark in black.

**Design**

Background: Solid cream colour, about #F2F0E8, with no gradient, photograph or pattern. Cream background (about #F2F0E8) with a faint pale yellow-beige legend panel (about #E9E7D8). Titles are near-black (#0B0B0B). Body and axis text is black (#111111). Accent colours: hot pink kicker square and kicker text (about #D6157A), pink highlight behind the phrase (about #FF8FB8), violet line and axis labels (about #7B1FE0), lavender area (about #C9A8FA). Headline is about 5.5% of slide height per line, chart subtitles about 2.6%, axis and legend labels about 2.0%, source and copyright about 1.8%. Chart gridlines are thin light grey rules (about #D6D3C7). Bars are flat with no outlines, no shadows, no gradients. Legend panel has rounded corners of about 8px radius.

INFRASTRUCTURE / As crypto reaches more mainstream users, the need for privacy is more urgent than ever / Privacy-related Google search interest¹ / Zcash supply in shielded (private) pools² / Total flows through Railgun (privacy application)³ / Notable privacy developments in 2025 / ✓ The Ethereum Foundation formed a new privacy team and announced a privacy-centric wallet SDK called Kohaku / ✓ Paxos announced a partnership with Aleo to launch USAD — a private, compliant stablecoin / ✓ Zcash wallet Zashi integrated with Near Intents to enable privacy-preserving, cross-chain swaps / ✓ Noir, a ZK programming language used for privacy applications, has seen an uptick in developer activity / ✓ OFAC officially lifted sanctions on Tornado Cash, the decentralized privacy protocol it had blacklisted in 2022 / Sources: 1/ Google Trends, 2/ ZecHub, 3/ Dune (@railgun_project) / ©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

**Layout**

Title block across the top with a small pink kicker above it, followed by a 2x2 grid of two chart panels per column: left column holds Google search interest (top) and Railgun flows (bottom), right column holds Zcash supply (top) and the 2025 developments list (bottom). Each panel heading sits above a full-width dark rule, left-aligned to the column edge; the footer runs along the bottom with the a16zcrypto logo at left, the sources line beside it, and the copyright line right-aligned at the bottom right. **Visuals**

Four panels plus five check icons. (1) Line chart titled Privacy-related Google search interest with superscript 1; y-axis 0 to 100 in steps of 25, no unit printed; x-axis years 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 with light vertical gridlines; five series: Blockchain privacy (purple), Crypto privacy (blue), Financial privacy (near-black navy), Private payments (light blue), Financial surveillance (orange-red). All series sit near 0 to 10 until about 2024, rise to about 10 to 20 in 2025, then spike sharply in late 2025: Crypto privacy peaks about 100, Financial privacy about 85, Private payments about 65, Financial surveillance about 20, and Blockchain privacy stays low; the legend box is inside the top-left of the plot. (2) Line chart titled Zcash supply in shielded (private) pools with superscript 2; y-axis 0 to 4M in steps of 1M; x-axis years 2018, 2020, 2022, 2024; one purple series rising from about 0 in 2017 to about 0.5M in 2020, about 0.9M in 2022, about 1.3M in 2024, then steep climb to about 3.8M by late 2025. (3) Bar chart titled Total flows through Railgun (privacy application) with superscript 3; y-axis $0 to $250M in steps of $50M; x-axis labels 2023, 2024, 2025 under groups of about 12 monthly navy bars; bars are roughly 0 to 15M in mid 2022, about 135M in early 2023, 20 to 90M through 2023, 50 to 60M in early 2024, rising to about 105M, 180M, 230M, 170M, 165M, 125M, 155M, 195M, 135M, 200M, 185M, 240M, 135M, 135M, 165M, 110M, 112M, 225M and about 185M by late 2025. (4) Five check icons, dark navy checkmarks with no circle, one beside each development item. (5) a16zcrypto wordmark logo in near-black at bottom left.

**Layout**

Single-slide timeline with a full-width header block: a small kicker at top left, a two-line bold headline left-aligned below it, and a subtitle beneath. Below that, a horizontal timeline axis runs across the slide at about 54% of the height, with 17 year markers; milestone blocks alternate above the axis (9 blocks) and below it (10 blocks), each left-aligned to its marker column in roughly 11% wide columns. The footer holds the a16zcrypto logo and source line at bottom left and the copyright line at bottom right, all on a margin of about 2.5% of the width.

**Visuals**

Horizontal timeline: a thin black line across the slide at about 54% of slide height with small solid pink (#E0147A) dots at 17 year positions, the last segment (after 2023+) becoming a pink arrowhead pointing right at x about 97%. From each dot a dotted vertical pink connector runs up to the upper milestone blocks or down to the lower blocks; the lower GGPR column extends furthest down to about 95% height. Year labels alternate: upper row 1990, 2007, 2010, 2013, 2016, 2019, 2020, 2022; lower row 1985, 1990s, 2008, 2011, 2014, 2017–2018, 2019–2020, 2019–2022, 2023+. Highlight: a soft pink (#FF9BC0) rectangle behind the words 'rapid acceleration in technical progress' in the headline. Small pink square icon before INFRASTRUCTURE. No photographs or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid warm off-white #F2EFE6. Warm off-white background (#F2EFE6). Headline and subtitle in near-black (#111111) bold geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style). Kicker and milestone headings in magenta-pink (#E0147A), bold sans-serif. Milestone body text in grey (#5A5A5A) regular sans-serif. Headline is about 5.9% of slide height, subtitle about 3.0%, milestone headings about 1.8%, body about 1.5%, kicker about 1.6% uppercase with letter-spacing. Lines are thin black (#111111) and pink. Rectangle highlight is pink (#FF9BC0) with square corners and no shadow. No gradients or shadows.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a cream background: a narrow left column holds the section label and the large headline, while the right column holds the chart title, the stacked area chart, its legend, a row of four equal rounded cards and a footer. The section label sits top-left at about 3% from the top and the headline starts below it at the left margin of about 2.3%. The footer has the a16z crypto logo bottom-left, the source line bottom-centre-left aligned to the card column, and the copyright bottom-right, all on one baseline near 95% of height. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Stacked area chart titled 'Total BTC at risk from quantum-vulnerable addresses'. Y axis is labelled 0, 2M, 4M, 6M, 8M (BTC, no unit label shown) with gridlines at each 2M step; X axis labelled 2011, 2013, 2015, 2017, 2019, 2021, 2023, 2025 with vertical gridlines at the labelled years. Two stacked series: bottom series 'At risk due to address re-use' in bright violet (about #8033F5) and top series 'At risk due to address type' in dark navy (about #242A45). Approximate values: 2011 re-use about 0.8M, total about 3.4M; 2012 re-use about 2.4M, total about 4.5M; 2016 re-use about 3.7M, total about 5.5M; 2018 re-use about 3.7M, total about 5.4M; 2020 re-use about 4.1M, total about 5.9M; 2022 re-use about 4.1M, total about 5.8M; 2024 re-use about 4.2M, total about 6.0M; final point about 4.8M re-use and about 6.65M total. A violet callout box in the lower right of the plot shows '6,650,280 Total BTC at risk' and '$746 billion Total USD at risk'. Legend below the chart: violet square then dark navy square with the two series labels. Below the chart are four cards, each with an icon: NIST wordmark with a circular US government seal; Bitcoin ₿ circle icon; Ethereum diamond circle icon; four blockchain icons (Solana-like, Cardano-like, Solana-stripes, and Near-like N) in black circles. Hyperlinks in pink are: transition, BIP-360, quantum-resistant signatures, structural advantage.

**Design**

Background: Solid cream colour #F1EFE6. Cream background (#F1EFE6). Section label and link text in hot pink #E0187F. Headline in heavy geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style), black #000000, with the phrase 'quantum-secure cryptography' highlighted by a pink #F58DB8 block behind the text. Chart title in semibold sans-serif black #000000. Body text in regular sans-serif black #000000. Card fills are a slightly darker beige #E8E4D6 with rounded corners of about 12px radius and no shadow. Chart gridlines light grey #C8C5BA, thin. Title text about 4.6% of slide height for the headline, body text about 1.6% of slide height.

Decorative graphic at top right: the numeral '06' in a very large bold sans-serif, set in two overlapping layers over a stepped orange block. The numeral's left and right parts sit partly over the orange block (rendered in off-white) and partly over the navy background (rendered in pale tan, about #D9CFBB), so the overlap creates a two-tone effect. The orange block is a stepped, notched rectangle with small rounded inner corners, filling about 61% to 93% of slide width and 14% to 50% of slide height. A green notched tab at top left (about 0% to 25% width and 0% to 15% height) holds the section label. No photographs, charts or icons apart from the wordmark logo.

Background: Solid navy #06205B. Flat navy background (#06205B) with no gradient or texture. Headline and section label in off-white (#F3F1EA); decorative numerals and tab accents use orange #F7941D, mint green #4DC98B and pale tan #D9CFBB. Headline and section label use a bold geometric grotesk sans-serif (Inter-like), all caps, tight tracking. Headline cap height is about 10% of slide height (font size about 14% of slide height). Section label is about 4.5% of slide height. Copyright line is a regular-weight sans-serif at about 2% of slide height. Tab and orange block have small corner radii (about 1% of slide width) and no shadows.

Four horizontal rows separated by thin dark full-width rules, each row split into three columns: a left column of plain text beside an exclamation icon, a middle column of bold text beside a check icon and logos, and a right column of dotted-outlined boxes holding statistics. **Text**

Calculated as the total number of VC deals categorized as “crypto” and “AI” divided by the total number categorized as “crypto”. / Source: Pitchbook / a16zcrypto / ©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a cream field: a left text column (title block, roughly the left 27% of the width) and a right chart column (roughly 32% to 97% of the width). The small section tag sits top-left at about 2% from the left and 3.5% from the top. The title starts at about 8.5% from the top and is left-aligned. The footer row sits at about 92% to 97% of the height: logo at far left, source line aligned with the chart's left edge, and copyright right-aligned to about 97% of the width. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

One filled area chart. Type: area chart with a solid orange fill and no outline on the top edge beyond the fill edge. Chart title: “Percent of crypto companies building in the “AI x crypto” category” with superscript footnote marker 1. Y axis: percent, 0% to 30%, gridlines at 0%, 10%, 20%, 30%, labels at left. X axis: years 2016 to 2025, labels shown at 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022, 2024 with vertical gridlines at each labelled year; baseline at 0% drawn as a solid black line. Series values (no values printed; estimates): 2016 about 12.3%, 2017 about 16.8%, 2018 about 13.9%, 2019 about 15.6%, 2020 about 17.3%, 2021 about 12.8%, 2022 about 14.2%, 2023 about 18.6%, 2024 about 20.0%, 2025 about 25.8%. No legend. No highlighted points, except a callout placed over the 2022 to 2023 area: a rounded light grey-beige rectangle (#E6E4D6, corner radius about 12px, no shadow) containing the OpenAI logo mark (black interlocked swirl icon) above the bold black label “ChatGPT Launches” in two centred lines. The callout overlaps the gridline at 20%. Icon: OpenAI blossom logo in black, about 50px. Logo at bottom-left: a16zcrypto wordmark in black lowercase.

**Design**

Background: Solid cream/off-white colour #F1EFE7. Warm off-white cream background with flat fills and no gradients or shadows. Title text is near-black #0A0A0A in a bold geometric sans-serif (Inter Bold style), body and axis text in black #000000 in Inter Regular. Accent colour: mint green #4FD98F for the section tag square and text and the highlight behind “convergence of crypto and AI”. Chart fill is orange #F5A030 at roughly 90% opacity. Title size about 5.2% of slide height (cap height about 3.7%); chart title about 2.6%; axis labels about 2.1%; footnote and source about 1.9%. Gridlines are thin light grey #CFCBC0 at 1px. Chart baseline is black 2px.

**Layout**

Two-column grid on a warm off-white field: a narrow left column holds the kicker and the large headline stacked from top-left, while the right column (about 31 to 96 percent of width) holds three equal-width pie charts in a row across the top and a full-width bar chart below a horizontal rule. Three pie charts and one grouped bar chart. Pie 1 titled '“AI native” company revenue share¹': green slice about 88% labelled '88% OpenAI and Anthropic' with OpenAI and Anthropic logos beneath; orange slice about 12% labelled '16 other top companies' with dotted leader lines; slice starts at 12 o'clock and runs counterclockwise. Pie 2 titled 'Cloud infrastructure market share²': green slice 63% labelled '63% Amazon, Microsoft, and Google' with Amazon, Microsoft and Google logos beneath; orange slice about 37% labelled 'All others'. Pie 3 titled 'Data center GPU market share³': green slice 94% labelled '94% NVIDIA' with NVIDIA logo beneath; orange slice about 6% labelled 'All others'. Pie slices have no outlines; dotted leader lines run from each label to its slice. Chart 'Net income growth⁴': clustered vertical bars, y-axis from -25% to 75% with gridlines at 75%, 50%, 25%, 0% and -25%; x-axis categories Q1'23 to Q4'25E. Green bars (Magnificent 7) about: Q1'23 6%, Q2'23 37%, Q3'23 54%, Q4'23 63%, Q1'24 52%, Q2'24 37%, Q3'24 22%, Q4'24 32%, Q1'25 29%, Q2'2E 26%, Q3'25E 23%, Q4'25E 12%. Values are estimates read from bar heights. Legend box at top of plot: light beige rounded rectangle containing green square 'Magnificent 7' followed by logos of NVIDIA, Microsoft, Apple, Google, Amazon, Meta and Tesla, then orange square 'Remaining 493 companies in S&P 500'. No highlighted points.

### Slide 47

**Text**

CRYPTO IN AMERICA
The U.S. has advanced key crypto legislation, bringing regulatory clarity to the industry
Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins (GENIUS)
Provides clear rules for stablecoin issuers
Bipartisan support in the Senate
50 Republicans, 18 Democrats in favor
Bipartisan support in the House
206 Republicans, 102 Democrats in favor
Signed into law
What does this new legislation do?
1. Establishes requirements for permitted stablecoin issuers
2. Defines reserve and backing standards
3. Provides transparency and disclosure rules, bolstering consumer protections
Digital Asset Market Clarity Act (CLARITY)
Establishes a new regulatory framework for digital asset markets
Bipartisan support in the House
216 Republicans, 78 Democrats in favor
Now progressing to the Senate
Not yet signed into law
What would this new legislation do?
1. Create a clear regulatory pathway for digital commodities
2. Enable oversight of blockchain-based intermediaries
3. Protect consumers with strong safeguards, while fostering innovation
a16zcrypto
Source: a16z crypto analysis
©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

**Layout**

Two equal columns sit side by side beneath a full-width title, with the left column for GENIUS and the right for CLARITY, each holding a heading, an italic subtitle, a rounded light card with a three-step flow, and a dark plum rounded box at its base. Text is left-aligned with a margin of about 2.3% on the left and right. The kicker sits top-left above the title, and the footer row holds the a16zcrypto logo and source line at left and the copyright at right; there is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Two vertical three-step flow diagrams, one per column. Left flow: peach circle with a double-check (bipartisan) icon, then a downward arrow, then a peach circle with a double-check icon, then a downward arrow, then a dark plum circle with a document-and-check-mark icon labelled Signed into law. Right flow: peach circle with double-check icon, then a solid downward arrow, then a peach circle with a dotted-circle icon labelled Now progressing to the Senate, then a dashed downward arrow, then an empty dashed-outline circle labelled Not yet signed into law. Vote counts are printed, not estimated: GENIUS Senate 50 Republicans and 18 Democrats in favor; GENIUS House 206 Republicans and 102 Democrats in favor; CLARITY House 216 Republicans and 78 Democrats in favor. Red #E0262E is used for Republican counts and blue #1A5CE0 for Democrat counts. Pink highlight rectangles (#E8A3F6) sit behind the words bringing regulatory and clarity in the title. No photographs.

**Design**

CRYPTO IN AMERICA
Federal agencies are already taking action as crypto legislation progresses
Legal actions
DOJ Deputy AG Blanche publishes a memorandum revising the DOJ’s crypto prosecution policies and priorities
SEC dismisses or settles court cases and investigations against crypto companies, including Binance, Coinbase, Consensys, Gemini, Kraken, OpenSea, Robinhood Crypto, and Uniswap Labs
Treasury reverses OFAC smart contract designation after federal court rules in plaintiffs’ favor
Rules, statements, and guidance
CFTC issues Advisory on foreign board of trade registration
DOJ Acting AAG delivers speech that “merely writing code, without ill-intent, is not a crime”
Federal Reserve, FDIC, and OCC rescind restrictive requirements for certain crypto activities
OCC announces actions to depoliticize the banking system
SEC issues statements and guidance on stablecoins, ETPs, staking, mining, and meme coins, among others
New initiatives
WH creates President’s Working Group on Digital Asset Markets and publishes policy recommendations report
CFTC announces “Crypto Sprint” and initiative on spot crypto asset contracts
SEC forms Crypto Task Force and launches “Project Crypto”
Treasury and the SEC host numerous crypto policy roundtables
Commerce begins using blockchains to publish GDP data
a16zcrypto

Three equal-width columns with tinted panels separated by thin vertical boundaries below a full-width title band; the title sits top-left spanning two lines, with a small section label above it. Bottom row of nine circular government seals, left to right: The White House seal reading THE WHITE HOUSE WASHINGTON (black and white building illustration); Commodity Futures Trading Commission seal (hexagonal, 1975); Department of Commerce seal reading UNITED STATES OF AMERICA (eagle and shield); Seal of the Department of Labor reading SEAL OF THE DEPARTMENT OF LABOR-type eagle emblem (1789-style); Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation seal reading FEDERAL DEPOSIT INSURANCE CORPORATION 1933; Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System seal; Office of the Comptroller of the Currency seal reading OFFICE OF THE COMPTROLLER OF THE CURRENCY; Securities and Exchange Commission seal reading SECURITIES AND EXCHANGE COMMISSION MCMXXXIV; Department of the Treasury seal reading DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY 1789. All seals are black line art on white, roughly 90 px diameter at this slide size.

**Layout**

Large stylised numerals '08' in the upper right, roughly x 62% to 99%, y 6% to 51%, built as overlapping blocky shapes with rounded corners. ### Slide 53

**Text**

WHAT'S NEXT FOR CRYPTO
Some of our thoughts for 2025 and beyond...
Market structure legislation will be a top policy priority for the industry and Congress to provide a clear regulatory framework for people building in crypto.
Stablecoin adoption will accelerate as network effects kick in across financial institutions, merchants, and consumers, strengthening the US dollar globally.
New innovation at the intersection of crypto and AI will begin to address the internet's most pressing challenges.
Traditional financial institutions and emerging fintechs will double down on crypto with new product launches, partnerships, and acquisitions.
Blockchain infrastructure will continue to improve, with throughput soon approaching that of today's largest internet services.
A new wave of real world assets will come onchain as crypto and tradfi continue to merge.
More talent will flow into crypto as the regulatory environment improves and interesting new opportunities for developers emerge.
More tokens will generate revenue by turning on fee switches, shifting the market's focus toward fundamentals.
New consumer products will bring the next wave of crypto users onchain.
a16zcrypto
©2025 a16z | All rights reserved worldwide

The cards have slightly irregular edges with small notched corners, so each card's top-left, top-right or bottom corner is cut in a step shape.

Two square-cornered thumbnail tiles and two beige arrow tiles in the left column, and a black rounded social panel on the right containing seven circular outline icons. The first thumbnail is a purple (#7D1A5B) square with a yellow concentric maze-like line pattern and the white-on-purple 'WEB3 a16z crypto' wordmark at its bottom; it is a logo tile with rounded corners of about 2% of width and no shadow. The second thumbnail is a solid orange (#FF6B1A) rounded square with a white stacked-lines and bookmark-tail icon (three horizontal white bars above a white downward-pointing ribbon shape), representing a newsletter. Each beige arrow tile (#E8E2C8, rounded corners about 2%) contains a black diagonal up-right arrow icon with a stroke of about 0.3% of slide height and an arrowhead at top-right. The seven social icons, left to right, are: X logo; LinkedIn 'in' logo; YouTube play-button logo; GitHub octocat logo; a Substack-style stylised 'n' logo; Spotify logo; Apple Podcasts icon (broadcast arcs around a microphone). Each icon is white and sits inside a thin grey (#5A5A5A) outlined circle about 5.1% of slide width in diameter, with about 1.4% gap between circles. The a16zcrypto wordmark at bottom-left is a black lowercase logotype. The lighting is flat; there are no photographs.
